```
1	# Staging Load Tests: Running Multiple Tests Simultaneously Causes 429s
2	
3	## Date: 2026-03-29
4	
5	## Context
6	
7	Running SDK load tests (`tests/load/`) against staging with 200 concurrent clients.
8	Initial runs hit 429 ThrottlerException, but investigation revealed the bypass mechanism works correctly.
9	
10	## Root Cause
11	
12	**Two load tests were launched simultaneously against the same staging instance** (sustained-load with 200 clients + BYO capacity ceiling with 50 clients). The combined load saturated the staging API. The throttle bypass header was being sent and accepted, but the server couldn't handle 250+ concurrent account creation flows.
13	
14	## How We Confirmed
15	
16	1. curl with bypass header: 20 concurrent -> all 200 OK
17	2. Node.js fetch with bypass header: 50 concurrent -> all 200 OK
18	3. vitest with `createClientPool(20)`: 20/20 in 4.7s
19	4. vitest with `createClientPool(50)`: 50/50 in 10.0s
20	5. Both tests together: 429s after ~10 accounts (server saturated)
21	
22	The bypass header skips rate limiter **guards**, but doesn't make the server infinitely scalable. The staging VPS has finite resources.
23	
24	## Rules
25	
26	- **Never run multiple load test scenarios concurrently against staging** -- run them sequentially
27	- The `tests/load/.env` must be sourced before running: `set -a && source .env && set +a`
28	- vitest does NOT auto-load `.env` files -- env must be sourced in the shell
29	
30	## Throttle Config Reference
31	
32	| Setting       | CI (NODE_ENV=test) | Staging (NODE_ENV=staging) |
33	| ------------- | ------------------ | -------------------------- |
34	| Short limit   | 200/sec            | 10/sec                     |
35	| Medium limit  | 2000/min           | 100/min                    |
36	| Bypass header | Works (unneeded)   | Works (required)           |
37	
38	## Key Files
39	
40	- `apps/api/src/common/guards/throttler-bypass.guard.ts` -- bypass logic
41	- `apps/api/src/app.module.ts` -- throttle limit config (lines 59-74)
42	- `tests/sdk-e2e/src/fixtures/test-harness.ts` -- account creation with bypass header
43	- `tests/load/src/harness/client-pool.ts` -- pool creation (batches of 5)

1	<!-- generated-by: gsd-doc-writer -->
2	
3	# @cipherbox/load-tests
4	
5	Load tests for the CipherBox API and SDK. Measures upload throughput, IPNS publish
6	concurrency, mixed workloads, sustained traffic, spike behaviour, and BYO-IPFS provider
7	capacity — all against a live API target.
8	
9	Part of the [CipherBox monorepo](../../README.md).
10	
11	## Tooling
12	
13	[Vitest](https://vitest.dev/) runs the scenarios sequentially (no parallelism). Each test
14	has a 10-minute timeout. Results are written to `metrics-*.json` files in this directory.
15	
16	## Scenarios
17	
18	| Scenario                | File                                          |
19	| ----------------------- | --------------------------------------------- |
20	| `upload-throughput`     | `src/scenarios/upload-throughput.test.ts`     |
21	| `ipns-publish-storm`    | `src/scenarios/ipns-publish-storm.test.ts`    |
22	| `mixed-workload`        | `src/scenarios/mixed-workload.test.ts`        |
23	| `sustained-load`        | `src/scenarios/sustained-load.test.ts`        |
24	| `spike-test`            | `src/scenarios/spike-test.test.ts`            |
25	| `byo-upload-throughput` | `src/scenarios/byo-upload-throughput.test.ts` |
26	| `byo-mixed-workload`    | `src/scenarios/byo-mixed-workload.test.ts`    |
27	| `byo-capacity-ceiling`  | `src/scenarios/byo-capacity-ceiling.test.ts`  |
28	
29	## Running Locally
30	
31	Set the required environment variables, then run a single scenario by name:
32	
33	```bash
34	LOAD_TEST_API_URL=http://localhost:3000 \
35	LOAD_TEST_SECRET=<test-login-secret> \
36	THROTTLE_BYPASS_SECRET=<throttle-bypass-secret> \
37	LOAD_TEST_CLIENTS=5 \
38	pnpm exec vitest run --no-coverage mixed-workload
39	```
40	
41	For BYO-IPFS scenarios, also set:
42	
43	```bash
44	BYO_IPFS_ENDPOINT=https://api.pinata.cloud
45	BYO_IPFS_AUTH_TOKEN=<token>
46	BYO_IPFS_PROTOCOL=pinata   # pinata | psa | kubo
47	BYO_IPFS_PROVIDER_NAME=pinata
48	```
49	
50	## Environment Variables
51	
52	| Variable                 | Required         | Description                                          |
53	| ------------------------ | ---------------- | ---------------------------------------------------- |
54	| `LOAD_TEST_API_URL`      | Yes              | Base URL of the API under test                       |
55	| `LOAD_TEST_SECRET`       | Yes              | `TEST_LOGIN_SECRET` value used to mint test sessions |
56	| `THROTTLE_BYPASS_SECRET` | Yes              | Secret to bypass API rate limiting during load runs  |
57	| `LOAD_TEST_CLIENTS`      | No (default `5`) | Number of concurrent virtual clients                 |
58	| `BYO_IPFS_ENDPOINT`      | byo-\* only      | BYO IPFS provider base URL                           |
59	| `BYO_IPFS_AUTH_TOKEN`    | byo-\* only      | Auth token for the BYO provider                      |
60	| `BYO_IPFS_PROTOCOL`      | byo-\* only      | Provider protocol: `pinata`, `psa`, or `kubo`        |
61	| `BYO_IPFS_PROVIDER_NAME` | byo-\* only      | Label used in metrics output                         |
62	
63	## CI
64	
65	The `load-test.yml` workflow (`manual dispatch only`) runs any scenario against `local` or
66	`staging`. For `local` it spins up Postgres, Kubo IPFS, and Redis service containers and
67	starts the API before executing. Metrics JSON is uploaded as a workflow artifact
68	(retained 30 days).
69	
70	## Related Docs
71	
72	- [Testing strategy](../TESTING_STRATEGY.md)
73	- [Full testing reference](../../docs/TESTING.md)
74	
```

1	# Performance Baselines - Phase 18
2	
3	## Capture Information
4	
5	| Field            | Value                                                           |
6	| ---------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------- |
7	| **Capture Date** | 2026-03-07T16:55:31Z (updated 2026-03-08)                       |
8	| **Environment**  | Staging (api-staging.cipherbox.cc)                              |
9	| **Kubo Version** | v0.34.0                                                         |
10	| **API Image**    | v0.24.2-staging-rc-1                                            |
11	| **VPS**          | Hostinger 76.13.151.200, 4 vCPU, 8GB RAM                        |
12	| **Script**       | `scripts/baseline-benchmark.sh` + `tests/e2e/load-test.spec.ts` |
13	| **Iterations**   | 20 measured + 3 warmup per operation (benchmark script)         |
14	| **File Size**    | 10KB random data (upload/download) for benchmark script         |
15	
16	## Methodology
17	
18	Baselines are captured using `scripts/baseline-benchmark.sh` which:
19	
20	1. Discovers the user's root IPNS name via `GET /vault`
21	2. Runs 3 warmup iterations (discarded) followed by 20 measured iterations
22	3. Measures client-side round-trip time via `curl -w "%{time_total}"`
23	4. Computes p50/p95/p99 from sorted timing values
24	5. IPNS Publish is excluded (requires signed record) -- captured from Prometheus
25	
26	Server-side histograms (`cipherbox_ipfs_ipns_duration_seconds`) provide internal timing without network overhead. Client-side timings from this script include network latency and are useful for end-to-end comparison.
27	
28	## Client-Side Timings (curl round-trip)
29	
30	| Operation           | p50    | p95    | p99    | Notes                                   |
31	| ------------------- | ------ | ------ | ------ | --------------------------------------- |
32	| IPNS Resolve        | 0.147s | 0.224s | 0.278s | `GET /ipns/resolve?ipnsName=<name>`     |
33	| IPNS Publish        | --     | --     | --     | See Prometheus (requires signed record) |
34	| IPFS Pin (upload)   | 0.138s | 0.218s | 0.227s | `POST /ipfs/upload` with 10KB file      |
35	| IPFS Cat (download) | 0.133s | 0.215s | 0.219s | `GET /ipfs/<cid>` for 10KB file         |
36	
37	## Server-Side Histograms (Prometheus)
38	
39	Captured from the API's `/metrics` endpoint via SSH on 2026-03-08. Includes cumulative data from the baseline-benchmark script run (single-client, ~38 ops) plus the 5-client load test (~377 ops across 5 concurrent users).
40	
41	### `cipherbox_ipfs_ipns_duration_seconds` — per-operation breakdown
42	
43	Percentiles computed from Prometheus histogram buckets (linear interpolation within bucket boundaries).
44	
45	| Operation        | Source  | Count | p50    | p95    | p99    | Mean   | Notes                                         |
46	| ---------------- | ------- | ----- | ------ | ------ | ------ | ------ | --------------------------------------------- |
47	| **publish**      | --      | 1367  | 180 ms | 519 ms | 904 ms | 196 ms | Kubo IPNS publish — dominant bottleneck       |
48	| **resolve**      | network | 529   | 135 ms | 284 ms | 488 ms | 126 ms | Kubo DHT lookup (cache miss)                  |
49	| **resolve**      | db      | 84    | 35 ms  | 93 ms  | 187 ms | 36 ms  | DB cache hit (success path)                   |
50	| **resolve** (fb) | db      | 239   | 23 ms  | 84 ms  | 230 ms | 32 ms  | DB cache fallback (Kubo resolve failed)       |
51	| **resolve**      | network | 42    | 231 ms | 650 ms | 930 ms | 251 ms | Network errors (Kubo resolve timeout/failure) |
52	| **pin**          | --      | 1923  | 8 ms   | 18 ms  | 31 ms  | 8 ms   | Kubo `pin add` — very fast for small files    |
53	| **cat**          | --      | 704   | 2 ms   | 5 ms   | 9 ms   | 2 ms   | Kubo `cat` — sub-5ms for cached content       |
54	
55	Note: Resolve has 4 series because the API tries Kubo first (source=network), then falls back to DB cache (source=db). The `result` label distinguishes success/error at the Kubo level.
56	
57	### Other histograms
58	
59	| Metric                                       | Status  | Notes                                      |
60	| -------------------------------------------- | ------- | ------------------------------------------ |
61	| `cipherbox_republish_batch_duration_seconds` | No data | Mock TEE provider doesn't report durations |
62	| `cipherbox_http_request_duration_seconds`    | Below   | Per-route breakdown in HTTP table          |
63	
64	## HTTP API Performance (from Prometheus)
65	
66	Response times by route — computed from `cipherbox_http_request_duration_seconds` histogram (routes with ≥10 requests):
67	
68	| Route                            | Count | p50    | p95    | p99    | Mean   | Notes                              |
69	| -------------------------------- | ----- | ------ | ------ | ------ | ------ | ---------------------------------- |
70	| `POST /ipfs/upload` [201]        | 1923  | 8 ms   | 45 ms  | 50 ms  | 13 ms  | Small–medium files (up to 500KB)   |
71	| `GET /ipfs/:cid` [200]           | 704   | 5 ms   | 10 ms  | 10 ms  | 2 ms   | Extremely fast server-side         |
72	| `GET /ipns/resolve` [200]        | 852   | 50 ms  | 245 ms | 467 ms | 91 ms  | Includes DB cache fallback path    |
73	| `GET /ipns/resolve` [404]        | 42    | 231 ms | 650 ms | 930 ms | 251 ms | IPNS name not found (new accounts) |
74	| `POST /ipns/publish` [201]       | 621   | 165 ms | 477 ms | 871 ms | 172 ms | Kubo IPNS publish — dominant cost  |
75	| `POST /ipns/publish-batch` [201] | 373   | 275 ms | 793 ms | 959 ms | 303 ms | TEE republish batch path           |
76	| `GET /vault` [200]               | 12    | 5 ms   | 9 ms   | 10 ms  | 3 ms   |                                    |
77	| `GET /vault/quota` [200]         | 838   | 5 ms   | 9 ms   | 10 ms  | 1 ms   |                                    |
78	| `GET /health` [200]              | 539   | 5 ms   | 10 ms  | 32 ms  | 4 ms   |                                    |
79	| `POST /auth/login` [200]         | 14    | 155 ms | 240 ms | 248 ms | 140 ms | JWT generation + Web3Auth verify   |
80	| `POST /auth/test-login` [200]    | 11    | 89 ms  | 229 ms | 246 ms | 104 ms |                                    |
81	| `POST /vault/init` [201]         | 12    | 5 ms   | 9 ms   | 10 ms  | 7 ms   |                                    |
82

84	
85	| Metric             | Value    | Notes                                                             |
86	| ------------------ | -------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------- |
87	| Peer Connections   | N/A      | Kubo v0.34.0 does not expose libp2p metrics to Prometheus         |
88	| Inbound Bandwidth  | N/A      | Grafana panel shows "No data"                                     |
89	| Outbound Bandwidth | N/A      | Grafana panel shows "No data"                                     |
90	| Memory Usage       | N/A      | Grafana panel shows "No data"                                     |
91	| Goroutines         | N/A      | Not exposed by Kubo                                               |
92	| Datastore Size     | ~320 MiB | From dashboard "Total Storage Used" stat (includes all user data) |
93	
94	## Load Test Details
95	
96	### Run 1: Manual stress test (single client)
97	
98	To populate initial IPNS publish histogram data, a manual stress test was performed via Playwright against the staging web app:
99	
100	| Operation     | Count      | Details                                                   |
101	| ------------- | ---------- | --------------------------------------------------------- |
102	| Folder create | 8          | stress-01 through -05, nested-subfolder, rapid-fire-01–03 |
103	| File upload   | 13         | 10 small (7–48 KB) + 3 large (1 MB, 5 MB, 10 MB)          |
104	| Rename        | 4          | 2 files, 1 folder, 1 large file                           |
105	| Move          | 3          | Files into stress-02, stress-03, renamed-folder-01        |
106	| Delete        | 10         | 5 files + 5 folders (some with contents)                  |
107	| **Total**     | **~38–43** | Each mutates metadata → IPNS publish                      |
108	
109	Concurrently, `baseline-benchmark.sh` ran 20 iterations of resolve/pin/cat (+ warmups).
110	
111	### Run 2: Automated load test (5 concurrent clients)
112	
113	`tests/e2e/load-test.spec.ts` — 5 clients with unique wallets, launched simultaneously (no staggering), each running ~70 file operations.
114	
115	| Metric              | Value                                       |
116	| ------------------- | ------------------------------------------- |
117	| **Clients**         | 5 (all started at the same time)            |
118	| **Total ops**       | 395 attempted, 377 succeeded (95.4%)        |
119	| **Failed ops**      | 18 (all timeouts — 30s per-op limit)        |
120	| **Total time**      | 317s (5.3 minutes)                          |
121	| **Throughput**      | 1.25 ops/sec (aggregate across all clients) |
122	| **IPNS publishes**  | ~377 (one per successful mutation)          |
123	| **Account cleanup** | 5/5 accounts deleted                        |
124	
125	Per-client breakdown:
126	
127	| Client | Succeeded | Failed | Notes                                         |
128	| ------ | --------- | ------ | --------------------------------------------- |
129	| C1     | 79        | 0      | Finished first — got head start before others |
130	| C2     | 72        | 7      | Warm-up timeout + move dialog timeouts        |
131	| C3     | 78        | 1      | Single upload timeout in images folder        |
132	| C4     | 73        | 6      | Warm-up timeout + folder creation timeout     |
133	| C5     | 75        | 4      | Warm-up timeout + batch delete timeout        |
134	
135	Failure pattern: C2–C5 all timed out on their warm-up folder operation (30s) while C1 raced ahead. Subsequent failures clustered around move dialog waits and folder navigation — consistent with server-side IPNS publish latency increasing under concurrent load. The 5-client workload drove ~1,300 IPNS publishes through Kubo simultaneously.
136	
137	## Notes
138	
139	- Client-side timings captured 2026-03-07 via `baseline-benchmark.sh` (5 runs × 20 iterations per operation = 100 data points per operation, 300 total across resolve/pin/cat)
140	- Server-side histogram values captured 2026-03-08 via SSH to staging VPS (`curl http://localhost:3000/metrics`) — cumulative across benchmark script + 5-client load test
141	- 5-client load test run 2026-03-08 via `tests/e2e/load-test.spec.ts` — 5 concurrent Playwright browsers, ~70 ops each, all launched simultaneously
142	- IPNS Publish is the dominant bottleneck at ~519ms p95 server-side (904ms p99) — essentially all time in Kubo, negligible HTTP overhead
143	- Under 5-client concurrency, publish-batch p95 reaches 793ms (vs 477ms for single publish) — batching multiple IPNS names increases latency
144	- IPNS Resolve network errors (n=42) have high latency (p50=231ms, p95=650ms) — these are Kubo timeouts that trigger DB cache fallback
145	- DB cache fallback is fast (p50=23ms) and handles 74% of resolve calls (239/323 db-path resolves were error-fallback)
146	- Pin and Cat remain extremely fast under load (p50=8ms and 2ms) — client-side timings (~130ms) are dominated by network latency
147	- Kubo v0.34.0 does not expose libp2p metrics — Kubo Health section is N/A
148	- TEE Republish Batch Duration shows "No data" — mock TEE provider doesn't report real durations
149	- These baselines will be compared against post-Phase 19 and Phase 22 measurements
150	- The benchmark script is deterministic: same iterations, same file size, same warmup count
151	- The load test script (`tests/e2e/load-test.spec.ts`) is parameterized: `LOAD_TEST_CLIENTS` env var (default: 5)
152	
153	## Comparison Target
154	
155	Phase 22 (after IPFS infrastructure changes) will re-run this benchmark and document:
156	
157	- Performance regression threshold: >20% p95 increase requires investigation
158	- Performance improvement targets vary by operation (see Phase 22 plan)
159

1	# Performance Baselines - Phase 19.2 (POST-Optimization)
2	
3	> These are POST-optimization baselines. Kubo datastore: pebbleds (LSM-tree). SDK upload flow: concurrent pins (Promise.allSettled for steps 2+3).
4	
5	## Capture Information
6	
7	| Field                   | Value                                                                                                                       |
8	| ----------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
9	| **Capture Date**        | 2026-03-23                                                                                                                  |
10	| **Environment**         | Local infrastructure (<docker-host>), API on localhost:3000                                                                 |
11	| **Kubo Version**        | v0.40.0                                                                                                                     |
12	| **Datastore**           | pebbleds (LSM-tree via `IPFS_PROFILE=server,pebbleds`)                                                                      |
13	| **SDK Version**         | Post-19.2 (file IPNS publish overlapped with folder metadata update/publish step)                                           |
14	| **Optimizations**       | 1) SDK concurrent pins (Promise.allSettled: file IPNS publish + folder metadata update/publish), 2) Kubo pebbleds datastore |
15	| **Datastore Confirmed** | `Datastore.Spec.type: "pebbleds"` verified via Kubo API                                                                     |
16	
17	## Upload Throughput Test (5 clients x 20 files = 100 uploads)
18	
19	| Metric               | Value             |
20	| -------------------- | ----------------- |
21	| **Scenario**         | Upload Throughput |
22	| **Clients**          | 5                 |
23	| **Total Ops**        | 100               |
24	| **Total Errors**     | 0                 |
25	| **Duration**         | 32.0s             |
26	| **Throughput**       | 3.12 ops/s        |
27	| **Data Transferred** | 27.1 MB           |
28	
29	### uploadFile Latency
30	
31	| Percentile | Latency |
32	| ---------- | ------- |
33	| **min**    | 551ms   |
34	| **p50**    | 1,502ms |
35	| **p95**    | 2,841ms |

40	## Mixed Workload Test (5 clients x 45 mixed ops)
41	
42	| Metric                 | Value          |
43	| ---------------------- | -------------- |
44	| **Scenario**           | Mixed Workload |
45	| **Clients**            | 5              |
46	| **Total Ops**          | 139            |
47	| **Total Errors**       | 139            |
48	| **Duration**           | 24.3s          |
49	| **Overall Throughput** | 5.72 ops/s     |
50	
51	### Operation Latencies
52	
53	| Operation    | Count | p50   | p95   | p99  | Throughput |
54	| ------------ | ----- | ----- | ----- | ---- | ---------- |
55	| createFolder | 40    | 500ms | 936ms | 1.2s | 1.65 ops/s |
56	| uploadFile   | 99    | 892ms | 1.8s  | 2.6s | 4.07 ops/s |
57	
58	**Note:** All 139 mixed workload operations reported errors (SDK-level failures caught by non-fatal error handling). Move, rename, and delete operations were skipped because they require previously created items which failed. This makes the mixed workload data unreliable for comparison -- see Caveats section below.
59	
60	## Before/After Comparison
61	
62	### Environment Caveat
63	
64	The pre-optimization baselines were captured against **staging** infrastructure (api-staging.cipherbox.cc, warm DHT, 4 vCPU VPS) while post-optimization baselines were captured against **local** infrastructure (localhost API, <docker-host> Docker host). These are different environments with different network characteristics, so percentage changes must be interpreted with caution.
65	
66	Additionally, the pre-optimization upload test used **10 clients** while this post-optimization test used **5 clients** (as specified in plan). More clients increases contention and latency, which means the pre-optimization numbers at 10 clients would be higher than at 5 clients.
67	
68	### Upload Throughput Comparison (primary metric)
69	
70	| Metric             | Pre-Optimization (staging, 10 clients) | Post-Optimization (local, 5 clients) | Change | Notes                               |
71	| ------------------ | -------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------ | ------ | ----------------------------------- |
72	| uploadFile p50     | 1,442ms                                | 1,502ms                              | +4.2%  | Within noise given environment diff |
73	| uploadFile p95     | 3,666ms                                | 2,841ms                              | -22.5% | Significant improvement at tail     |
74	| uploadFile p99     | 4,044ms                                | 3,432ms                              | -15.1% | Tail latency reduced                |
75	| uploadFile avg     | 1,649ms                                | 1,613ms                              | -2.2%  | Essentially flat                    |
76	| throughput (ops/s) | 5.87                                   | 3.12                                 | -46.8% | NOT comparable: 10 vs 5 clients     |
77	| errors             | 0                                      | 0                                    | --     | Both error-free                     |
78	
79	### Mixed Workload Comparison (limited validity)
80	
81	| Metric           | Pre-Optimization (staging, 5 clients) | Post-Optimization (local, 5 clients) | Change | Notes                                  |
82	| ---------------- | ------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------ | ------ | -------------------------------------- |
83	| createFolder p50 | 511ms                                 | 500ms                                | -2.2%  | Similar                                |
84	| uploadFile p50   | 613ms                                 | 892ms                                | +45.5% | Post-opt data unreliable (all errored) |
85	| mixed throughput | 9.32 ops/s                            | 5.72 ops/s                           | -38.6% | Post-opt data unreliable (all errored) |
86	
87	**Mixed workload comparison is NOT valid:** The post-optimization run had 100% error rate across all operations, meaning the latency numbers reflect error-path timing (fast failures), not successful operation timing. The pre-optimization mixed workload numbers came from staging with zero errors.
88	
89	### Server-Side Latency Analysis
90	
91	From the pre-optimization Prometheus data, a single `uploadFile` makes 5 sequential API calls totaling ~5.4s server-side:
92	
93	| Step      | API Call                        | Pre-Optimization | Expected Post-Optimization | Optimization Applied        |
94	| --------- | ------------------------------- | ---------------- | -------------------------- | --------------------------- |
95	| 1         | POST /ipfs/upload (ciphertext)  | 1.73s            | 1.73s (unchanged)          | pebbleds may reduce         |
96	| 2         | POST /ipfs/upload (metadata)    | 1.73s            | Concurrent with step 3     | SDK concurrent pins         |
97	| 3         | POST /ipns/publish-batch        | 0.11s            | Concurrent with step 2     | SDK concurrent pins         |
98	| 4         | POST /ipfs/upload (folder meta) | 1.73s            | 1.73s (unchanged)          | pebbleds may reduce         |
99	| 5         | POST /ipns/publish              | 0.14s            | 0.14s (unchanged)          | None                        |
100	| **Total** |                                 | **~5.4s**        | **~3.7s expected**         | Save ~1.7s from concurrency |
101	
102	The observed p50 of 1.5s (5 clients, local) is well below the pre-optimization server-side estimate of 5.4s. This confirms that both optimizations (concurrent pins + pebbleds) are contributing to reduced per-upload latency.
103	
104	## Three-Point Local Comparison (Matched Environment)
105	
106	### Methodology
107	
108	All three measurement runs used the same local environment: API on localhost:3000, Docker services (IPFS/Kubo v0.40.0, PostgreSQL, Redis, someguy) on <docker-host> with someguy delegated routing (<docker-host>:8190). Each run used 50 concurrent clients uploading 20 files each (1KB-500KB random sizes, 1,000 total operations). The 50-client count was determined by a concurrency probe (Run 0) as the highest stable count with zero errors. Each run isolates one optimization variable:
109	
110	- **Run 1 (No Opts):** Sequential SDK upload flow + flatfs datastore (pre-optimization baseline)
111	- **Run 2 (SDK Concurrent):** Concurrent SDK pins (Promise.allSettled for steps 2+3) + flatfs datastore
112	- **Run 3 (SDK + Pebbleds):** Concurrent SDK pins + pebbleds datastore (LSM-tree)
113	
114	Rate limit bypass was applied to all runs (LOAD_TEST_SECRET + THROTTLE_BYPASS_SECRET passed inline, API running with NODE_ENV=test).
115	
116	### Concurrency Probe (Run 0)
117	
118	Before the three-point comparison, a concurrency probe established the local infrastructure's concurrency ceiling using the sequential SDK + flatfs configuration:
119	
120	| Clients | uploadFile p50 | p95      | p99      | throughput ops/s | errors         |
121	| ------- | -------------- | -------- | -------- | ---------------- | -------------- |
122	| 1       | 131ms          | 287ms    | 497ms    | 6.36             | 0              |
123	| 5       | 1,016ms        | 1,871ms  | 2,148ms  | 4.97             | 0              |
124	| 10      | 2,499ms        | 3,768ms  | 4,496ms  | 3.95             | 0              |
125	| 20      | 5,897ms        | 8,806ms  | 9,741ms  | 3.43             | 0              |
126	| 50      | 14,789ms       | 19,315ms | 21,861ms | 3.36             | 0              |
127	| 75      | 30,804ms       | 53,603ms | 59,892ms | 2.44             | 10             |
128	| 100     | --             | --       | --       | --               | many (timeout) |
129	
130	**Chosen N: 50 clients** -- the highest client count with zero errors and no timeout failures. At 75 clients, 10 errors appeared; at 100 clients, widespread timeouts occurred. The 50-client count also satisfies SC3's requirement to measure throughput at scale.
131	
132	### Three-Point Results Table (50 clients x 20 files = 1,000 uploads)
133	
134	| Metric           | Run 1 (No Opts) | Run 2 (SDK Concurrent) | Run 3 (SDK + Pebbleds) | SDK Delta (R2 vs R1) | Pebbleds Delta (R3 vs R2) | Total Delta (R3 vs R1) |
135	| ---------------- | --------------- | ---------------------- | ---------------------- | -------------------- | ------------------------- | ---------------------- |
136	| uploadFile p50   | 14,664ms        | 14,664ms               | 13,860ms               | 0.0%                 | -5.5%                     | **-5.5%**              |
137	| uploadFile p95   | 18,734ms        | 44,704ms               | 16,302ms               | +138.5%              | -63.5%                    | **-13.0%**             |
138	| uploadFile p99   | 20,519ms        | 50,301ms               | 17,467ms               | +145.1%              | -65.3%                    | **-14.9%**             |
139	| uploadFile avg   | 14,791ms        | 20,061ms               | 13,984ms               | +35.6%               | -30.3%                    | **-5.5%**              |
140	| uploadFile min   | 5,463ms         | 7,194ms                | 5,453ms                | +31.7%               | -24.2%                    | -0.2%                  |
141	| uploadFile max   | 22,379ms        | 53,422ms               | 19,466ms               | +138.7%              | -63.6%                    | -13.0%                 |
142	| throughput ops/s | 3.45            | 2.54                   | 3.69                   | -26.4%               | +45.3%                    | **+7.0%**              |
143	| total duration   | 289.8s          | 394.0s                 | 271.1s                 | +35.9%               | -31.2%                    | -6.5%                  |
144	| data transferred | 250.2 MB        | 246.3 MB               | 244.7 MB               | --                   | --                        | --                     |
145	| errors           | 0               | 0                      | 0                      | --                   | --                        | --                     |
146	| datastore        | flatfs          | flatfs                 | pebbleds               | --                   | --                        | --                     |
147	| SDK mode         | sequential      | concurrent             | concurrent             | --                   | --                        | --                     |
148	
149	### Optimization Attribution
150	
151	**SDK concurrent pins alone (Run 2 vs Run 1): DEGRADED performance at 50 clients.**
152	
153	The concurrent SDK change had a counter-intuitive negative effect at high concurrency:
154	
155	- p50 unchanged (0.0%), indicating median behavior is dominated by the sequential IPFS pin operations (steps 1, 4, 5) which were not parallelized
156	- p95 increased by +138.5% (18.7s to 44.7s) -- concurrent metadata pin + IPNS publish doubled the simultaneous write load on flatfs, causing severe contention at the tail
157	- Throughput dropped -26.4% (3.45 to 2.54 ops/s) -- the increased tail latency held up client slots, reducing overall throughput
158	- Total test duration increased 36% (290s to 394s)
159	
160	**Conclusion:** Concurrent SDK pins without a matching datastore improvement creates more write contention than it saves in sequential wait time, especially under high concurrency where flatfs's file-per-block storage becomes an I/O bottleneck.
161	
162	**Pebbleds datastore (Run 3 vs Run 2): RESCUED performance and pushed past baseline.**
163	
164	Switching from flatfs to pebbleds while keeping concurrent SDK pins active produced dramatic improvements:
165	
166	- p95 dropped -63.5% (44.7s to 16.3s) -- pebbleds' LSM-tree batched writes absorbed the concurrent pin load that overwhelmed flatfs
167	- p99 dropped -65.3% (50.3s to 17.5s) -- near-elimination of the extreme tail latency caused by flatfs contention
168	- Throughput increased +45.3% (2.54 to 3.69 ops/s) -- the fastest of all three runs
169	
170	**Combined effect (Run 3 vs Run 1): The optimizations are synergistic, not additive.**
171	
172	- p50 reduced -5.5% (14,664ms to 13,860ms) -- modest median improvement
173	- p95 reduced -13.0% (18,734ms to 16,302ms) -- meaningful tail improvement
174	- p99 reduced -14.9% (20,519ms to 17,467ms) -- consistent tail improvement
175	- Throughput improved +7.0% (3.45 to 3.69 ops/s)
176	
177	The key finding is that **SDK concurrent pins REQUIRE pebbleds to deliver any benefit**. The two optimizations exhibit a mandatory synergy: concurrent pins generate more simultaneous write I/O, and only pebbleds (with its batched LSM writes) can handle that load without contention-induced tail blow-up. Deploying concurrent pins without pebbleds would be a performance regression.
178	
179	### Definitive Success Criteria Analysis (Three-Point)
180	
181	This section provides the definitive SC analysis using matched-environment data. The earlier staging-vs-local comparison above was preliminary and confounded by environment and client-count mismatches.
182	
183	#### SC1: Per-upload server time reduced to ~3.5s p50 (from ~5.4s)
184	
185	**NOT MET at 50 clients -- but target was based on different conditions.** The three-point comparison at 50 clients shows p50 of 13.9s (Run 3) vs 14.7s (Run 1), a 5.5% improvement. The original ~5.4s figure came from Prometheus server-side measurements at lower concurrency on staging. At 50 concurrent clients, per-upload time is dominated by queueing and resource contention, not the sequential API call chain.
186	
187	For reference, the concurrency probe shows p50 at 1 client = 131ms and at 5 clients = 1,016ms, both well below the 3.5s target. The SC1 target is achievable at lower concurrency levels. At high concurrency (50 clients), the bottleneck shifts from sequential API calls to IPFS infrastructure throughput, which both optimizations address at the tail (p95/p99) rather than the median.
188	
189	#### SC2: 50-client scaling behavior
190	
191	**OBSERVABLE from the concurrency probe.** The probe shows clear scaling behavior:
192	
193	| Clients | p50      | Throughput | p50 per additional client |
194	| ------- | -------- | ---------- | ------------------------- |
195	| 1       | 131ms    | 6.36 ops/s | --                        |
196	| 5       | 1,016ms  | 4.97 ops/s | +221ms/client             |
197	| 10      | 2,499ms  | 3.95 ops/s | +297ms/client             |
198	| 20      | 5,897ms  | 3.43 ops/s | +340ms/client             |
199	| 50      | 14,789ms | 3.36 ops/s | +296ms/client             |
200	
201	Throughput degrades gracefully from 6.36 ops/s (1 client) to 3.36 ops/s (50 clients) -- a 47% reduction over a 50x increase in concurrency. The system remains stable with zero errors up to 50 clients. At 75 clients, errors begin appearing, establishing the local infrastructure ceiling. With pebbleds, 50-client throughput improves to 3.69 ops/s (Run 3), indicating better scaling under load.
202	
203	#### SC3: >15% throughput increase at matched client count
204	
205	**NOT MET.** The three-point comparison at 50 clients shows +7.0% throughput improvement (3.45 to 3.69 ops/s). While the improvement is consistent and error-free, it falls short of the 15% target.
206	
207	However, the improvement is entirely attributable to the pebbleds datastore. SDK concurrent pins alone caused a -26.4% regression. The +7.0% combined improvement represents the net effect after pebbleds overcomes the concurrent-pins contention penalty and adds its own I/O efficiency gains.
208	
209	The tail latency improvements are more substantial: p95 -13.0%, p99 -14.9%. The optimizations provide more benefit at the tail than at the median, which is valuable for user experience (fewer slow outliers) even if median throughput improvement is modest.
210	
211	#### SC4: Before/after documented with matched environment
212	
213	**MET.** This three-point local comparison section provides the definitive matched-environment before/after analysis. All three runs used identical infrastructure (localhost API, <docker-host> Docker host, someguy routing), identical client count (50), and identical test scenario (upload-throughput, 20 files per client, 1KB-500KB). The only variables changed between runs were SDK mode and datastore type.
214	
215	## Success Criteria Analysis (Preliminary)
216	
217	> **Note:** The analysis below was the original preliminary assessment using mismatched environments (staging vs local) and client counts (10 vs 5). See the "Definitive Success Criteria Analysis (Three-Point)" section above for the authoritative assessment.
218	
219	### SC1: Per-upload server time reduced to ~3.5s p50 (from ~5.4s)
220	
221	**Likely MET but requires staging Prometheus validation.** The client-side p50 of 1.5s at 5 clients is significantly below the target. However, the pre-optimization 5.4s figure was measured server-side via Prometheus histograms on staging under higher concurrency (10-200 clients). A true apples-to-apples comparison requires running the same Prometheus analysis on staging with pebbleds deployed.
222	
223	The 1.5s client-side p50 at 5 clients includes:
224	
225	- Network round-trips (localhost, minimal)
226	- Client-side encryption
227	- All 5 API calls (now with steps 2+3 concurrent)
228	
229	This is consistent with the optimization removing ~1.7s from the sequential path.
230	
231	### SC3: >15% throughput increase at 5 clients
232	
233	**Cannot be validated with current data.** The pre-optimization baseline used 10 clients on staging (5.87 ops/s) and the post-optimization used 5 clients on localhost (3.12 ops/s). These are not comparable due to:
234	
235	1. Different client counts (throughput scales with clients)
236	2. Different environments (staging VPS vs local)
237	3. Different rate limiting configurations
238	
239	**SC3 validation requires:** Running load tests on staging after pebbleds deployment, using the same client count and environment.
240	
241	### SC2 & SC4: Deferred to Staging
242	
243	- SC2 (50-client scaling) requires staging environment with relaxed rate limits
244	- SC4 (Prometheus histograms) requires staging Grafana dashboards
245	
246	## Caveats
247	
248	1. **Environment mismatch (preliminary comparison):** Pre-optimization baselines from staging; post-optimization from local. Direct percentage comparisons are indicative, not definitive. The three-point local comparison above resolves this.
249	2. **Client count mismatch (preliminary comparison):** Pre-optimization upload test used 10 clients; post-optimization used 5. Throughput is not directly comparable. The three-point comparison uses 50 clients consistently.
250	3. **Mixed workload invalid:** 100% error rate in post-optimization mixed workload run makes that data unsuitable for comparison. Root cause likely rate limiting or SDK concurrent operation conflicts under mixed workload patterns.
251	4. **Rate limiting:** Local API running in development mode (not NODE_ENV=test) applies tighter rate limits, causing 429 errors at 10 clients. The 5-client upload test completed cleanly. Three-point runs used NODE_ENV=test with bypass secrets.
252	5. **Concurrent pins + flatfs regression:** SDK concurrent pins without pebbleds cause a performance regression at 50 clients. Both optimizations must be deployed together.
253	6. **SC3 not met at 50 clients:** Throughput improvement is +7.0%, below the 15% target. Tail latency improvements (p95 -13.0%, p99 -14.9%) are more substantial.
254	
255	## 75-Client Stress Test (Bonus Run)
256	
257	To test whether pebbleds extends the infrastructure's concurrency ceiling, a bonus run was performed at 75 clients — the level where the flatfs probe failed with 10 errors and a 600s timeout.
258	
259	| Metric           | 75 clients (sequential + flatfs, probe) | 75 clients (concurrent + pebbleds, bonus) | Change         |
260	| ---------------- | --------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------- | -------------- |
261	| uploadFile p50   | 30,804ms                                | 21,969ms                                  | **-28.7%**     |
262	| uploadFile p95   | 53,603ms                                | 25,316ms                                  | **-52.8%**     |
263	| uploadFile p99   | 59,892ms                                | 26,879ms                                  | **-55.1%**     |
264	| uploadFile avg   | 32,354ms                                | 21,901ms                                  | **-32.3%**     |
265	| throughput ops/s | 2.44                                    | 3.51                                      | **+43.9%**     |
266	| errors           | 10                                      | **0**                                     | **eliminated** |
267	| status           | timed out (600s)                        | **clean (427s)**                          | **fixed**      |
268	
269	**Key finding:** Pebbleds doesn't just improve performance at high concurrency — it eliminates the failure mode entirely. At 75 clients, flatfs produced auth token expiry cascades (401s → "Key wrapping failed" → "Folder not loaded"), while pebbleds completed all 1500 ops with zero errors. The throughput improvement of +43.9% at 75 clients far exceeds the +7.0% seen at 50 clients, suggesting pebbleds' benefits scale with concurrency pressure.
270	
271	This result also satisfies **SC3 (>15% throughput improvement)** at the 75-client level, even though it was not met at 50 clients.
272

1	/**
2	 * Load Test Threshold Checking
3	 *
4	 * Defines pass/fail thresholds for load test scenarios.
5	 * Compares observed metrics against defined limits and produces
6	 * actionable violation messages for CI output.
7	 */
8	
9	import type { OperationMetrics } from './metrics';
10	
11	export interface ThresholdConfig {
12	  /** Operation name to match against OperationMetrics.operation */
13	  operation: string;
14	  /** Maximum allowed p95 latency in milliseconds */
15	  p95MaxMs: number;
16	  /** Maximum allowed error rate (0.0 to 1.0) */
17	  errorRateMax: number;
18	}
19	
20	export interface ThresholdResult {
21	  passed: boolean;
22	  violations: string[];
23	}
24	
25	/**
26	 * Check collected metrics against defined thresholds.
27	 * Returns pass/fail with descriptive violation messages.
28	 *
29	 * Operations in thresholds that are not found in metrics are silently
30	 * skipped — a scenario may not exercise every operation.
31	 */
32	export function checkThresholds(
33	  metrics: OperationMetrics[],
34	  thresholds: ThresholdConfig[]
35	): ThresholdResult {
36	  const violations: string[] = [];
37	
38	  for (const t of thresholds) {
39	    const m = metrics.find((op) => op.operation === t.operation);
40	    if (!m) continue;
41	
42	    if (m.latency.p95 > t.p95MaxMs) {
43	      violations.push(
44	        `${t.operation} p95 ${Math.round(m.latency.p95)}ms exceeds threshold ${t.p95MaxMs}ms`
45	      );
46	    }
47	
48	    const errorRate = m.count > 0 ? m.errors / m.count : 0;
49	    if (errorRate > t.errorRateMax) {
50	      violations.push(
51	        `${t.operation} error rate ${(errorRate * 100).toFixed(1)}% exceeds threshold ${(t.errorRateMax * 100).toFixed(1)}%`
52	      );
53	    }
54	  }
55	
56	  return { passed: violations.length === 0, violations };
57	}
58	
59	/**
60	 * Assert all thresholds pass, logging violations on failure.
61	 * Throws with a descriptive message if any threshold is violated.
62	 */
63	export function expectThresholdsPassed(
64	  metrics: OperationMetrics[],
65	  thresholds: ThresholdConfig[]
66	): void {
67	  const result = checkThresholds(metrics, thresholds);
68	  if (!result.passed) {
69	    console.warn('THRESHOLD VIOLATIONS:');
70	    result.violations.forEach((v) => console.warn(`  - ${v}`));
71	    throw new Error(`Threshold violations:\n${result.violations.join('\n')}`);
72	  }
73	}
74

> AGENT

Found it. Here's the full picture.

## The load test: `.github/workflows/load-test.yml` ("Load Tests")

Manual-dispatch-only workflow that runs the `tests/load/` vitest scenarios against a **live** API target. Two targets: `local` (spins up Postgres/Kubo/Redis + API as services) or `staging` (hits the deployed `STAGING_API_URL`).

**Where it was built** — the instrumentation you're remembering came in across these phases:
- **Phase 18** (`performance-instrumentation`) — Prometheus histograms + first staging baselines
- **Phase 19.2** (`ipfs-upload-performance-optimization`) — pebbleds datastore, the staging CI load-test scaling tables
- **Phase 21** (`byo-ipfs-node-support`) — the `byo-*` scenarios
- **Phase 22** (`performance-baselines-completion`) + **Phase 34** (`e2e-test-expansion-staging-baselines`) — baseline completion

## How to fire it against staging

Code under test = whatever is **deployed** to staging (target is `STAGING_API_URL`), not the workflow ref. So **run it after your deploy lands**, otherwise you'll measure the old build. Dispatch from `main`:

```bash
env -u GITHUB_TOKEN gh workflow run load-test.yml \
  -f environment=staging \
  -f scenario=upload-throughput \
  -f client_count=200
```

Staging secrets/vars are already wired (`STAGING_API_URL`, `STAGING_TEST_LOGIN_SECRET`, `STAGING_THROTTLE_BYPASS_SECRET`) and it runs under the `staging` GitHub environment — **the run may pause for environment approval**.

**Critical gotcha** (from `.learnings/2026-03-29-staging-load-test-throttle-bypass.md`): never run two staging scenarios concurrently — combined load saturates the 4-vCPU VPS and you get false 429s. The workflow's `concurrency: load-test-staging` + `cancel-in-progress: false` enforces queueing, so just dispatch them one at a time.

## What to compare against (regression anchors)

| Scenario | Anchor | Key baseline numbers |
|---|---|---|
| `upload-throughput` @ 50/100/200 | 19.2 staging CI table | **15.10 / 18.76 / 19.22 ops/s**, 0/0/58 errors |
| `sustained-load` @ 200 | `tests/load/baselines/staging-sustained-load.json` (2026-03-29) | uploadFile p95 **11.6s**, createFolder p95 **11.9s**, 0.17% error rate |
| `byo-capacity-ceiling` | `staging-byo-capacity-ceiling.json` | ⚠️ all-error (Pinata free-tier quota exhausted) — **skip, not useful for regression** |

Pass/fail thresholds (`tests/load/src/harness/thresholds.ts`): the run **fails** if `uploadFile p95 > 10s` or `createFolder p95 > 5s` — note the existing sustained-load baseline already trips both at 200 clients, so a 200-client run is expected to "fail" the threshold gate; judge by the metrics artifact (`metrics-*.json`, retained 30 days), not the green/red.

## Recommendation

Best signal for "did recent API changes regress perf" is **`upload-throughput` at matched 50 / 100 / 200 clients** vs the 19.2 staging table — same workflow, same VPS, directly comparable. Then `sustained-load` @ 200 vs the March baseline. Run them sequentially.

Want me to dispatch these (in order, after you confirm the deploy is live), watch the runs, pull the metrics artifacts, and diff them against the baselines for you? I'll hold until you say the staging deploy is done.

3	 *

7	
8	import { describe, it, afterAll } from 'vitest';

1	/**
2	 * File Upload/Download Workload
3	 *
4	 * Parameterized workload that uploads and downloads files of varying sizes.
5	 * Individual operation failures are recorded as errors but don't abort the workload.
6	 */
7	
8	import type { PoolClient } from '../harness/client-pool';
9	
10	export interface FileWorkloadOptions {
11	  /** Number of files to upload */
12	  fileCount: number;
13	  /** Min file size in bytes */
14	  minSize: number;
15	  /** Max file size in bytes */
16	  maxSize: number;
17	  /** Whether to verify downloads after upload */
18	  verifyDownloads: boolean;
19	}
20	
21	/**
22	 * Run a file upload workload on a single client.
23	 */
24	export async function runFileWorkload(pc: PoolClient, opts: FileWorkloadOptions): Promise<void> {
25	  const { fileCount, minSize, maxSize, verifyDownloads } = opts;
26	  if (minSize < 0 || maxSize < minSize) {
27	    throw new Error(`Invalid size bounds: minSize=${minSize}, maxSize=${maxSize}`);
28	  }
29	  const { client, rootIpnsName, rootFolderKey, metrics } = pc;
30	
31	  for (let i = 0; i < fileCount; i++) {
32	    const size = minSize + Math.floor(Math.random() * (maxSize - minSize));
33	    const data = new Uint8Array(size);
34	    // crypto.getRandomValues() has a 65536 byte limit per call
35	    for (let offset = 0; offset < size; offset += 65536) {
36	      const chunk = new Uint8Array(data.buffer, offset, Math.min(65536, size - offset));
37	      crypto.getRandomValues(chunk);
38	    }
39	    const fileName = `load-${pc.id}-file-${i}-${size}b.bin`;
40	
41	    try {
42	      // Upload
43	      await metrics.measure(
44	        'uploadFile',
45	        () => client.uploadFile(rootIpnsName, data, fileName, 'application/octet-stream'),
46	        size
47	      );
48	
49	      // Optionally download and verify
50	      if (verifyDownloads) {
51	        const folder = client.getFolderTree().get(rootIpnsName);
52	        const child = folder?.children.find((c) => c.name === fileName);
53	        const fileIpnsName =
54	          child && 'fileMetaIpnsName' in child ? child.fileMetaIpnsName : undefined;
55	        await metrics.measure(
56	          'downloadFile',
57	          async () => {
58	            if (!fileIpnsName) {
59	              throw new Error(
60	                `[Client ${pc.id}] File "${fileName}" not found in folder state after upload`
61	              );
62	            }
63	            const downloaded = await client.downloadFromIpns(fileIpnsName, rootFolderKey);
64	            if (downloaded.length !== data.length) {
65	              throw new Error(
66	                `[Client ${pc.id}] Size mismatch: uploaded ${data.length}, downloaded ${downloaded.length}`
67	              );
68	            }
69	            // Byte-level content verification
70	            if (Buffer.compare(Buffer.from(data), Buffer.from(downloaded)) !== 0) {
71	              throw new Error(
72	                `[Client ${pc.id}] Content mismatch for "${fileName}" (same size, different bytes)`
73	              );
74	            }
75	            return downloaded;
76	          },
77	          size
78	        );
79	      }
80	    } catch (err) {
81	      console.warn(
82	        `[Client ${pc.id}] File upload ${fileName} failed: ${(err as Error).message?.slice(0, 150)}`
83	      );
84	    }
85	  }
86	}
87

1	/**
2	 * Load Test Client Pool
3	 *
4	 * Manages N CipherBoxClient instances, each with its own test account.
5	 * Creates accounts in parallel, distributes workloads, and collects metrics.
6	 *
7	 * Reuses the shared createTestAccount/deleteTestAccount from sdk-e2e
8	 * to avoid duplicating the 5-step account provisioning sequence.
9	 */
10	
11	import {
12	  createTestAccount,
13	  deleteTestAccount,
14	  type TestAccount,
15	} from '../../../sdk-e2e/src/fixtures/test-harness';
16	import { MetricsCollector, type OperationMetrics } from './metrics';
17	import { printSummary, toJsonReport } from './reporter';
18	import {
19	  KuboProvider,
20	  PsaProvider,
21	  PinataProvider,
22	  type PinningProvider,
23	  type PinningMode,
24	  type ExternalProviderConfig,
25	  type SdkContext,
26	} from '@cipherbox/sdk-core';
27	import { createAxiosInstance, type ApiClientConfig } from '@cipherbox/api-client';
28	
29	const API_URL = process.env.LOAD_TEST_API_URL ?? 'http://localhost:3000';
30	const SECRET = process.env.LOAD_TEST_SECRET ?? 'e2e-test-secret-do-not-use-in-production';
31	const THROTTLE_BYPASS = process.env.THROTTLE_BYPASS_SECRET ?? '';
32	
33	/** BYO external provider config from environment variables */
34	export const BYO_ENDPOINT = process.env.BYO_IPFS_ENDPOINT; // e.g., https://api.pinata.cloud/psa
35	export const BYO_AUTH_TOKEN = process.env.BYO_IPFS_AUTH_TOKEN ?? '';
36	export const BYO_PROTOCOL = (process.env.BYO_IPFS_PROTOCOL ?? 'kubo') as 'kubo' | 'psa' | 'pinata';
37	export const BYO_PROVIDER_NAME = process.env.BYO_IPFS_PROVIDER_NAME ?? 'external';
38	
39	export interface PoolClient extends TestAccount {
40	  id: number;
41	  metrics: MetricsCollector;
42	}
43	
44	export interface ClientPoolOptions {
45	  clientCount: number;
46	  label: string;
47	}
48	
49	/** Options for creating a pool of BYO-mode clients */
50	export interface ByoPoolOptions extends ClientPoolOptions {
51	  /** External provider config -- all BYO clients share this provider */
52	  externalProvider: ExternalProviderConfig;
53	  /** Pinning mode for BYO clients */
54	  pinningMode: 'external' | 'dual';
55	}
56	
57	/** A pool client augmented with BYO provider and mode */
58	export interface ByoPoolClient extends PoolClient {
59	  provider: PinningProvider;
60	  pinningMode: PinningMode;
61	  externalProvider: ExternalProviderConfig;
62	}
63	
64	/**
65	 * Create a pool of N authenticated CipherBoxClient instances.
66	 *
67	 * Each client has its own test account, vault, metrics collector, and
68	 * instance-scoped axios (no shared singleton).
69	 */
70	export async function createClientPool(opts: ClientPoolOptions): Promise<PoolClient[]> {
71	  const { clientCount, label } = opts;
72	  console.log(`Creating ${clientCount} test accounts for "${label}"...`);
73	  const start = performance.now();
74	
75	  // Create accounts in parallel batches of 5 to avoid overwhelming the API
76	  const clients: PoolClient[] = [];
77	  const batchSize = 5;
78	
79	  for (let batch = 0; batch < clientCount; batch += batchSize) {
80	    const batchEnd = Math.min(batch + batchSize, clientCount);
81	    const promises = [];
82	
83	    for (let i = batch; i < batchEnd; i++) {
84	      promises.push(
85	        createTestAccount({
86	          apiUrl: API_URL,
87	          secret: SECRET,
88	          label: `${label}-${i}`,
89	          emailPrefix: 'load',
90	        }).then((account): PoolClient => ({ ...account, id: i, metrics: new MetricsCollector() }))
91	      );
92	    }
93	
94	    const results = await Promise.allSettled(promises);
95	    for (const result of results) {
96	      if (result.status === 'fulfilled') {
97	        clients.push(result.value);
98	      } else {
99	        console.warn(`Failed to create pool client: ${result.reason}`);
100	      }
101	    }
102	  }
103	
104	  const elapsed = performance.now() - start;
105	  console.log(
106	    `Created ${clients.length}/${clientCount} clients in ${(elapsed / 1000).toFixed(1)}s`
107	  );
108	
109	  return clients;
110	}
111	
112	/**
113	 * Destroy all clients in the pool and delete test accounts.
114	 */
115	export async function destroyClientPool(pool: PoolClient[]): Promise<void> {
116	  console.log(`Cleaning up ${pool.length} test accounts...`);
117	  for (const pc of pool) {
118	    pc.client.destroy();
119	    // Zero harness-owned key material (client.destroy() only clears its internal copies)
120	    pc.privateKey.fill(0);
121	    pc.rootFolderKey.fill(0);
122	    pc.rootIpnsKeypair.privateKey.fill(0);
123	    await deleteTestAccount(pc, API_URL);
124	  }
125	}
126	
127	/**
128	 * Create a pool of N BYO-mode authenticated CipherBoxClient instances.
129	 *
130	 * Each client has its own test account plus a PinningProvider instance
131	 * (KuboProvider or PsaProvider) for external pinning.
132	 *
133	 * Returns an empty array (with warning) if BYO_IPFS_ENDPOINT is not set,
134	 * allowing scenarios to skip gracefully.
135	 */
136	export async function createByoClientPool(opts: ByoPoolOptions): Promise<ByoPoolClient[]> {
137	  if (!BYO_ENDPOINT) {
138	    console.warn(
139	      'BYO_IPFS_ENDPOINT not set -- skipping BYO pool creation. ' +
140	        'Set BYO_IPFS_ENDPOINT, BYO_IPFS_AUTH_TOKEN, and optionally BYO_IPFS_PROTOCOL to run BYO scenarios.'
141	    );
142	    return [];
143	  }
144	
145	  const basePool = await createClientPool({
146	    clientCount: opts.clientCount,
147	    label: opts.label,
148	  });
149	
150	  const byoPool: ByoPoolClient[] = basePool.map((pc) => {
151	    let provider: PinningProvider;
152	    switch (opts.externalProvider.protocol) {
153	      case 'psa':
154	        provider = new PsaProvider(opts.externalProvider.endpoint, opts.externalProvider.authToken);
155	        break;
156	      case 'pinata':
157	        provider = new PinataProvider(
158	          opts.externalProvider.endpoint,
159	          opts.externalProvider.authToken
160	        );
161	        break;
162	      case 'kubo':
163	      default:
164	        provider = new KuboProvider(
165	          opts.externalProvider.endpoint,
166	          opts.externalProvider.authToken
167	        );
168	        break;
169	    }
170	
171	    return {
172	      ...pc,
173	      provider,
174	      pinningMode: opts.pinningMode,
175	      externalProvider: opts.externalProvider,
176	    };
177	  });
178	
179	  // Enable BYO mode on each account's vault so register-cid is authorized
180	  const byoResults = await Promise.allSettled(
181	    byoPool.map(async (pc) => {
182	      const ctx = createSdkContext(pc);
183	      await ctx.axiosInstance!.patch('/vault/byo-status', { isByo: true });
184	      return pc;
185	    })
186	  );
187	
188	  const enabledPool: ByoPoolClient[] = [];
189	  for (const result of byoResults) {
190	    if (result.status === 'fulfilled') {
191	      enabledPool.push(result.value);
192	    } else {
193	      console.warn(`BYO enable failed for a client: ${result.reason}`);
194	    }
195	  }
196	
197	  if (enabledPool.length === 0) {
198	    throw new Error('Failed to enable BYO mode on any client — cannot run BYO scenarios');
199	  }
200	  if (enabledPool.length < byoPool.length) {
201	    console.warn(
202	      `BYO mode enabled on ${enabledPool.length}/${byoPool.length} clients — continuing with partial pool`
203	    );
204	  }
205	
206	  console.log(
207	    `BYO pool: ${enabledPool.length} clients with ${opts.externalProvider.protocol} provider ` +
208	      `(mode: ${opts.pinningMode}, endpoint: ${opts.externalProvider.endpoint})`
209	  );
210	
211	  return enabledPool;
212	}
213	
214	/**
215	 * Re-authenticate a test account via /auth/test-login.
216	 * Returns the new access token.
217	 */
218	async function reAuthenticate(email: string): Promise<string> {
219	  const headers: Record<string, string> = { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' };
220	  if (THROTTLE_BYPASS) {
221	    headers['X-Throttle-Bypass'] = THROTTLE_BYPASS;
222	  }
223	
224	  const res = await fetch(`${API_URL}/auth/test-login`, {
225	    method: 'POST',
226	    headers,
227	    body: JSON.stringify({ email, secret: SECRET }),
228	  });
229	
230	  if (!res.ok) {
231	    throw new Error(`Re-auth failed (${res.status}): ${await res.text()}`);
232	  }
233	
234	  const { accessToken } = (await res.json()) as { accessToken: string };
235	  return accessToken;
236	}
237	
238	/**
239	 * Create an SdkContext for headless sdk-core calls from a PoolClient.
240	 *
241	 * Constructs a fresh axiosInstance with 401 interceptor so that
242	 * headless workloads get automatic token refresh.
243	 */
244	export function createSdkContext(pc: PoolClient): SdkContext {
245	  // Mutable token holder -- updated by 401 interceptor
246	  let currentToken = pc.accessToken;
247	
248	  // Shared refresh promise to coalesce concurrent 401 re-auths
249	  let refreshPromise: Promise<string> | null = null;
250	
251	  const config: ApiClientConfig = {
252	    baseUrl: API_URL,
253	    getAccessToken: async () => currentToken,
254	    refreshAccessToken: async () => {
255	      if (!refreshPromise) {
256	        refreshPromise = reAuthenticate(pc.email).finally(() => {
257	          refreshPromise = null;
258	        });
259	      }
260	      const newToken = await refreshPromise;
261	      currentToken = newToken;
262	      pc.accessToken = newToken;
263	      return newToken;
264	    },
265	    defaultHeaders: THROTTLE_BYPASS ? { 'X-Throttle-Bypass': THROTTLE_BYPASS } : undefined,
266	  };
267	
268	  const axiosInstance = createAxiosInstance(config);
269	
270	  return {
271	    apiUrl: API_URL,
272	    getAccessToken: async () => currentToken,
273	    axiosInstance,
274	  };
275	}
276	
277	/**
278	 * Aggregate metrics from all pool clients, print summary, and return metrics.
279	 * Extracts the repeated boilerplate from every load test scenario.
280	 */
281	export async function aggregateAndReport(
282	  scenarioName: string,
283	  pool: PoolClient[]
284	): Promise<OperationMetrics[]> {
285	  const allMetrics = new MetricsCollector();
286	
287	  let minTimestamp = Infinity;
288	  let maxTimestamp = 0;
289	
290	  for (const pc of pool) {
291	    for (const sample of pc.metrics.getReadonlySamples()) {
292	      allMetrics.record(sample);
293	      if (sample.timestamp < minTimestamp) minTimestamp = sample.timestamp;
294	      if (sample.timestamp > maxTimestamp) maxTimestamp = sample.timestamp;
295	    }
296	  }
297	
298	  // Set collector timing from sample timestamps for accurate throughput calculation
299	  allMetrics.setElapsedMs(maxTimestamp > minTimestamp ? maxTimestamp - minTimestamp : 1);
300	
301	  const totalDuration = maxTimestamp > minTimestamp ? maxTimestamp - minTimestamp : 0;
302	  const metrics = allMetrics.getMetrics();
303	
304	  printSummary(scenarioName, metrics, totalDuration, pool.length);
305	
306	  // Write JSON report to file for CI artifact upload
307	  const reportJson = toJsonReport(scenarioName, metrics, totalDuration, pool.length);
308	  console.log('\nJSON Report:\n' + reportJson);
309	
310	  try {
311	    const fs = await import('node:fs/promises');
312	    const slug = scenarioName.toLowerCase().replace(/[^a-z0-9]+/g, '-');
313	    await fs.writeFile(`metrics-${slug}.json`, reportJson);
314	  } catch (err) {
315	    console.warn(`Failed to write metrics report: ${(err as Error).message}`);
316	  }
317	
318	  return metrics;
319	}
320	
```

1	/**
2	 * SDK-Core Headless Workloads
3	 *
4	 * Direct sdk-core function calls without CipherBoxClient overhead.
5	 * Used for bottleneck isolation in load test scenarios.
6	 */
7	
8	import * as sdkCore from '@cipherbox/sdk-core';
9	import type { SdkContext } from '@cipherbox/sdk-core';
10	import type { PoolClient } from '../harness/client-pool';
11	import { createSdkContext } from '../harness/client-pool';
12	
13	export interface SdkWorkloadClient {
14	  pc: PoolClient;
15	  ctx: SdkContext;
16	}
17	
18	/** Prepare a PoolClient for headless sdk-core workloads. */
19	export function prepareSdkClient(pc: PoolClient): SdkWorkloadClient {
20	  return { pc, ctx: createSdkContext(pc) };
21	}
22	
23	/**
24	 * IPNS publish + resolve cycle workload.
25	 *
26	 * Each cycle publishes a new IPNS record then resolves it.
27	 * Sequence numbers start at 100 to avoid conflicting with real vault state.
28	 */
29	export async function runIpnsPublishWorkload(
30	  swc: SdkWorkloadClient,
31	  opts: { cycles: number }
32	): Promise<void> {
33	  const { pc, ctx } = swc;
34	  for (let i = 0; i < opts.cycles; i++) {
35	    // Publish
36	    try {
37	      await pc.metrics.measure('sdkIpnsPublish', () =>
38	        sdkCore.createAndPublishIpnsRecord({
39	          ipnsPrivateKey: pc.rootIpnsKeypair.privateKey,
40	          ipnsName: pc.rootIpnsName,
41	          metadataCid: `bafybeig${Date.now().toString(36)}${i.toString(36)}`,
42	          sequenceNumber: BigInt(i + 100),
43	          ctx,
44	        })
45	      );
46	    } catch (err) {
47	      console.warn(
48	        `[Client ${pc.id}] IPNS publish ${i} failed: ${(err as Error).message?.slice(0, 150)}`
49	      );
50	    }
51	
52	    // Resolve
53	    try {
54	      await pc.metrics.measure('sdkIpnsResolve', () =>
55	        sdkCore.resolveIpnsRecord(pc.rootIpnsName, ctx)
56	      );
57	    } catch (err) {
58	      console.warn(
59	        `[Client ${pc.id}] IPNS resolve ${i} failed: ${(err as Error).message?.slice(0, 150)}`
60	      );
61	    }
62	  }
63	}
64	
65	/**
66	 * Upload pipeline workload (encrypt + pin + file metadata IPNS publish).
67	 *
68	 * Calls sdkCore.uploadFile directly, bypassing CipherBoxClient folder tree
69	 * management to isolate the upload pipeline performance.
70	 */
71	export async function runUploadPipelineWorkload(
72	  swc: SdkWorkloadClient,
73	  opts: { fileCount: number; fileSizeBytes: number }
74	): Promise<void> {
75	  const { pc, ctx } = swc;
76	  for (let i = 0; i < opts.fileCount; i++) {
77	    const data = new Uint8Array(opts.fileSizeBytes);
78	    crypto.getRandomValues(data);
79	
80	    try {
81	      const result = await pc.metrics.measure(
82	        'sdkUploadFile',
83	        () =>
84	          sdkCore.uploadFile({
85	            ctx,
86	            folderKey: pc.rootFolderKey,
87	            userPublicKey: pc.publicKey,
88	            data,
89	            fileId: crypto.randomUUID(),
90	            mimeType: 'application/octet-stream',
91	          }),
92	        opts.fileSizeBytes
93	      );
94	      result.fileKey.fill(0);
95	    } catch (err) {
96	      console.warn(
97	        `[Client ${pc.id}] Upload ${i} failed: ${(err as Error).message?.slice(0, 150)}`
98	      );
99	    } finally {
100	      data.fill(0);
101	    }
102	  }
103	}
104	
105	/**
106	 * Folder metadata read workload (IPNS resolve + IPFS fetch + decrypt).
107	 *
108	 * Uses loadFolderMetadata which resolves IPNS, fetches the CID,
109	 * and decrypts the folder metadata -- the full read path.
110	 */
111	export async function runFolderReadWorkload(
112	  swc: SdkWorkloadClient,
113	  opts: { cycles: number }
114	): Promise<void> {
115	  const { pc, ctx } = swc;
116	  for (let i = 0; i < opts.cycles; i++) {
117	    try {
118	      await pc.metrics.measure('sdkFolderRead', () =>
119	        sdkCore.loadFolderMetadata({
120	          ipnsName: pc.rootIpnsName,
121	          folderKey: pc.rootFolderKey,
122	          ctx,
123	        })
124	      );
125	    } catch (err) {
126	      console.warn(
127	        `[Client ${pc.id}] Folder read ${i} failed: ${(err as Error).message?.slice(0, 150)}`
128	      );
129	    }
130	  }
131	}
132

1	/**
2	 * Sustained Load Scenario
3	 *
4	 * N clients running at a steady rate for a configurable duration.
5	 * Tests latency stability over time — detects memory leaks, connection
6	 * pool exhaustion, and other time-dependent degradation.
7	 */
8	
9	import { describe, it, afterAll } from 'vitest';
10	import {
11	  createClientPool,
12	  destroyClientPool,
13	  aggregateAndReport,
14	  type PoolClient,
15	} from '../harness/client-pool';
16	import { expectThresholdsPassed, type ThresholdConfig } from '../harness/thresholds';
17	
18	const NUM_CLIENTS = parseInt(process.env.LOAD_TEST_CLIENTS ?? '5', 10);
19	const DURATION_SEC = parseInt(process.env.LOAD_TEST_DURATION ?? '300', 10);
20	const OPS_PER_SEC_PER_CLIENT = 2;
21	
22	describe('Sustained Load', () => {
23	  let pool: PoolClient[] = [];
24	
25	  afterAll(async () => {
26	    await destroyClientPool(pool);
27	  });
28	
29	  it(`${NUM_CLIENTS} clients × ${OPS_PER_SEC_PER_CLIENT} ops/sec for ${DURATION_SEC}s`, async () => {
30	    pool = await createClientPool({
31	      clientCount: NUM_CLIENTS,
32	      label: 'sustained',
33	    });
34	
35	    const endTime = Date.now() + DURATION_SEC * 1000;
36	
37	    const results = await Promise.allSettled(
38	      pool.map((pc) => runSustainedClient(pc, endTime, OPS_PER_SEC_PER_CLIENT))
39	    );
40	
41	    const succeeded = results.filter((r) => r.status === 'fulfilled').length;
42	    console.log(`\nClients completed: ${succeeded}/${pool.length}`);
43	
44	    const metrics = await aggregateAndReport('Sustained Load', pool);
45	
46	    // Threshold check: same as upload-throughput for sustained operations
47	    const THRESHOLDS: ThresholdConfig[] = [
48	      { operation: 'uploadFile', p95MaxMs: 10_000, errorRateMax: 0.05 },
49	      { operation: 'createFolder', p95MaxMs: 5_000, errorRateMax: 0.05 },
50	    ];
51	
52	    expectThresholdsPassed(metrics, THRESHOLDS);
53	  });
54	});
55	
56	async function runSustainedClient(
57	  pc: PoolClient,
58	  endTime: number,
59	  opsPerSec: number
60	): Promise<void> {
61	  const { client, rootIpnsName, metrics } = pc;
62	  metrics.start();
63	
64	  const intervalMs = 1000 / opsPerSec;
65	  let folderCounter = 0;
66	
67	  while (Date.now() < endTime) {
68	    const opStart = Date.now();
69	
70	    try {
71	      // Alternate between folder create and delete to keep state manageable
72	      const name = `sustained-${pc.id}-${folderCounter++}`;
73	
74	      const folder = await metrics.measure('createFolder', () =>
75	        client.createFolder(rootIpnsName, name)
76	      );
77	
78	      // Small file upload
79	      const data = new Uint8Array(1024);
80	      crypto.getRandomValues(data);
81	      await metrics.measure(
82	        'uploadFile',
83	        () => client.uploadFile(rootIpnsName, data, `${name}.bin`, 'application/octet-stream'),
84	        1024
85	      );
86	
87	      // Cleanup to prevent state bloat
88	      const folderState = client.getFolderTree().get(rootIpnsName);
89	      const fileChild = folderState?.children.find((c) => c.name === `${name}.bin`);
90	      if (fileChild) {
91	        await metrics.measure('deleteItem', () => client.deleteItem(rootIpnsName, fileChild.id));
92	      }
93	      await metrics.measure('deleteItem', () => client.deleteItem(rootIpnsName, folder.id));
94	    } catch {
95	      // Non-fatal — continue the sustained load
96	    }
97	
98	    // Wait for next interval
99	    const elapsed = Date.now() - opStart;
100	    const wait = Math.max(0, intervalMs - elapsed);
101	    if (wait > 0) {
102	      await new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, wait));
103	    }
104	  }
105	
106	  metrics.stop();
107	}
108

1	/**
2	 * Mixed Workload
3	 *
4	 * Simulates realistic usage: a mix of folder CRUD, file uploads,
5	 * renames, moves, and deletes with weighted probabilities.
6	 */
7	
8	import type { FolderChild } from '@cipherbox/core';
9	import type { PoolClient } from '../harness/client-pool';
10	
11	export interface MixedWorkloadOptions {
12	  /** Total number of operations */
13	  totalOps: number;
14	  /** Operation weights (sum doesn't need to be 1, they're normalized) */
15	  weights?: {
16	    createFolder?: number;
17	    uploadFile?: number;
18	    renameItem?: number;
19	    moveItem?: number;
20	    deleteItem?: number;
21	  };
22	}
23	
24	const DEFAULT_WEIGHTS = {
25	  createFolder: 2,
26	  uploadFile: 4,
27	  renameItem: 1,
28	  moveItem: 1,
29	  deleteItem: 1,
30	};
31	
32	/**
33	 * Run a mixed workload on a single client.
34	 */
35	export async function runMixedWorkload(pc: PoolClient, opts: MixedWorkloadOptions): Promise<void> {
36	  const { client, rootIpnsName, metrics } = pc;
37	  const weights = { ...DEFAULT_WEIGHTS, ...opts.weights };
38	  const values = Object.values(weights);
39	  if (values.some((w) => typeof w !== 'number' || w < 0 || !Number.isFinite(w))) {
40	    throw new Error(`Invalid weights: all values must be non-negative finite numbers`);
41	  }
42	  const totalWeight = values.reduce((a, b) => a + b, 0);
43	  if (totalWeight <= 0) {
44	    throw new Error(`Invalid weights: total weight must be > 0`);
45	  }
46	
47	  // Track created items for rename/move/delete targets
48	  const folderIds: Array<{
49	    id: string;
50	    ipnsName: string;
51	    folderKey: Uint8Array;
52	    ipnsPrivateKey: Uint8Array;
53	  }> = [];
54	  const fileIds: Array<{ id: string; name: string }> = [];
55	
56	  for (let i = 0; i < opts.totalOps; i++) {
57	    const roll = Math.random() * totalWeight;
58	    let cumulative = 0;
59	
60	    // Pick operation based on weighted random
61	    if ((cumulative += weights.createFolder) > roll) {
62	      // Create folder
63	      try {
64	        const name = `mix-folder-${pc.id}-${i}`;
65	        const folder = await metrics.measure('createFolder', () =>
66	          client.createFolder(rootIpnsName, name)
67	        );
68	        folderIds.push(folder);
69	      } catch {
70	        /* non-fatal */
71	      }
72	    } else if ((cumulative += weights.uploadFile) > roll) {
73	      // Upload file
74	      try {
75	        const size = 1024 + Math.floor(Math.random() * 50_000);
76	        const data = new Uint8Array(size);
77	        crypto.getRandomValues(data);
78	        const fileName = `mix-file-${pc.id}-${i}.bin`;
79	
80	        await metrics.measure(
81	          'uploadFile',
82	          () => client.uploadFile(rootIpnsName, data, fileName, 'application/octet-stream'),
83	          size
84	        );
85	
86	        const folder = client.getFolderTree().get(rootIpnsName);
87	        const child = folder?.children.find((c: FolderChild) => c.name === fileName);
88	        if (child) fileIds.push({ id: child.id, name: fileName });
89	      } catch {
90	        /* non-fatal */
91	      }
92	    } else if ((cumulative += weights.renameItem) > roll) {
93	      // Rename a random file
94	      if (fileIds.length > 0) {
95	        const idx = Math.floor(Math.random() * fileIds.length);
96	        const target = fileIds[idx];
97	        const newName = `${target.name}-r${i}`;
98	        try {
99	          await metrics.measure('renameItem', () =>
100	            client.renameItem(rootIpnsName, target.id, newName)
101	          );
102	          fileIds[idx] = { ...target, name: newName };
103	        } catch {
104	          /* non-fatal */
105	        }
106	      }
107	    } else if ((cumulative += weights.moveItem) > roll) {
108	      // Move a file into a folder (if both exist)
109	      if (fileIds.length > 0 && folderIds.length > 0) {
110	        const fileIdx = Math.floor(Math.random() * fileIds.length);
111	        const folderIdx = Math.floor(Math.random() * folderIds.length);
112	        const targetFolder = folderIds[folderIdx];
113	
114	        // Ensure target folder is registered
115	        if (!client.hasFolder(targetFolder.ipnsName)) {
116	          client.registerFolder(
117	            targetFolder.ipnsName,
118	            targetFolder.folderKey,
119	            { publicKey: new Uint8Array(0), privateKey: targetFolder.ipnsPrivateKey },
120	            [],
121	            1n
122	          );
123	        }
124	
125	        try {
126	          await metrics.measure('moveItem', () =>
127	            client.moveItem(rootIpnsName, targetFolder.ipnsName, fileIds[fileIdx].id)
128	          );
129	          fileIds.splice(fileIdx, 1); // Remove from root tracking
130	        } catch {
131	          /* non-fatal */
132	        }
133	      }
134	    } else {
135	      // Delete a random item
136	      if (fileIds.length > 0) {
137	        const idx = Math.floor(Math.random() * fileIds.length);
138	        try {
139	          await metrics.measure('deleteItem', () =>
140	            client.deleteItem(rootIpnsName, fileIds[idx].id)
141	          );
142	          fileIds.splice(idx, 1);
143	        } catch {
144	          /* non-fatal */
145	        }
146	      } else if (folderIds.length > 0) {
147	        const idx = Math.floor(Math.random() * folderIds.length);
148	        try {
149	          await metrics.measure('deleteItem', () =>
150	            client.deleteItem(rootIpnsName, folderIds[idx].id)
151	          );
152	          folderIds.splice(idx, 1);
153	        } catch {
154	          /* non-fatal */
155	        }
156	      }
157	    }
158	  }
159	}
160

7	import { describe, it, afterAll } from 'vitest';
